</p><p>Since 1983, Ivory Coast's <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital</a> has been Yamoussoukro, while Abidjan was the administrative center. Most countries maintain their embassies in Abidjan.
</p><p>Although most of the fighting ended by late 2004, the country remained split in two, with the north controlled by the New Forces. A new presidential election was expected to be held in October 2005, and the rival parties agreed in March 2007 to proceed with this, but it continued to be postponed until November 2010 due to delays in its preparation.
</p><p>Elections were finally <a href="/wiki/Ivorian_presidential_election,_2010" class="mw-redirect" title="Ivorian presidential election, 2010">held in 2010</a>. The first round of elections was held peacefully and widely hailed as free and fair. Runoffs were held on 28 November 2010, after being delayed one week from the original date of 21 November. <a href="/wiki/Laurent_Gbagbo" title="Laurent Gbagbo">Laurent Gbagbo</a> as president ran against former Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Alassane_Ouattara" title="Alassane Ouattara">Alassane Ouattara</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> On 2 December, the Electoral Commission declared that Ouattara had won the election by a margin of 54% to 46%. In response, the Gbagbo-aligned Constitutional Council rejected the declaration, and the government announced that country's borders had been sealed. An Ivorian military spokesman said, "The air, land, and sea border of the country are closed to all movement of people and goods."<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>President Alassane Ouattara has led the country since 2010 and he was re-elected to a third term in November 2020 <a href="/wiki/2020_Ivorian_presidential_election" title="2020 Ivorian presidential election">elections</a> boycotted by two leading opposition figures former President Henri Konan Bedie and ex-Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Pascal_Affi_N%27Guessan" title="Pascal Affi N&#39;Guessan">Pascal Affi N'Guessan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Achi_II_government" title="Achi II government">Achi II government</a> has ruled the country since April 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:IC_Gbagbo_Motta_eng_195.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/35/IC_Gbagbo_Motta_eng_195.jpg" decoding="async" width="189" height="195" class="mw-file-element" data-file-width="189" data-file-height="195" /></a><figcaption>Former President <a href="/wiki/Laurent_Gbagbo" title="Laurent Gbagbo">Laurent Gbagbo</a> was extradited to the <a href="/wiki/International_Criminal_Court" title="International Criminal Court">International Criminal Court</a> (ICC), becoming the first head of state to be taken into the court's custody.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Foreign relations of Ivory Coast">Foreign relations of Ivory Coast</a></div>
<p>In Africa, Ivorian diplomacy favors step-by-step economic and political cooperation. In 1959, Ivory Coast formed the Council of the Entente with Dahomey (Benin), Upper Volta (Burkina Faso), <a href="/wiki/Niger" title="Niger">Niger</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Togo" title="Togo">Togo</a>; in 1965, the <a href="/wiki/African_and_Malagasy_Common_Organization" class="mw-redirect" title="African and Malagasy Common Organization">African and Malagasy Common Organization</a> (OCAM); in 1972, the Economic Community of West Africa (CEAO). The latter organization changed to the <a href="/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Economic Community of West African States">Economic Community of West African States</a> (ECOWAS) in 1975. A founding member of the Organization of African Unity (OAU) in 1963 and then of the <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a> in 2000, Ivory Coast defends respect for state sovereignty and peaceful cooperation between African countries.
</p><p>Worldwide, Ivorian diplomacy is committed to fair economic and trade relations, including the fair trade of agricultural products and the promotion of peaceful relations with all countries. Ivory Coast thus maintains diplomatic relations with international organizations and countries all around the world. In particular, it has signed United Nations treaties such as the Convention Relating to the Status of Refugees, the 1967 Protocol, and the 1969 Convention Governing Specific Aspects of Refugee Problems in Africa. Ivory Coast is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_of_Islamic_Cooperation" title="Organisation of Islamic Cooperation">Organisation of Islamic Cooperation</a>, African Union, <a href="/wiki/La_Francophonie" class="mw-redirect" title="La Francophonie">La Francophonie</a>, <a href="/wiki/Latin_Union" title="Latin Union">Latin Union</a>, <a href="/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Economic Community of West African States">Economic Community of West African States</a>, and <a href="/wiki/South_Atlantic_Peace_and_Cooperation_Zone" title="South Atlantic Peace and Cooperation Zone">South Atlantic Peace and Cooperation Zone</a>.
</p><p>Ivory Coast has partnered with nations of the Sub-Saharan region to strengthen water and sanitation <a href="/wiki/Infrastructure" title="Infrastructure">infrastructure</a>. This has been done mainly with the help of organizations such as UNICEF and corporations like Nestle.<sup id="cite_ref-unicef_65-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unicef-65">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2015, the United Nations engineered the <a href="/wiki/Sustainable_Development_Goals" title="Sustainable Development Goals">Sustainable Development Goals</a> (replacing the Millennium Development Goals). They focus on health, education, poverty, hunger, climate change, water sanitation, and hygiene. A major focus was clean water and salinization. Experts working in these fields have designed the <a href="/wiki/WASH" title="WASH">WASH</a> concept. WASH focuses on safe drinkable water, hygiene, and proper sanitation. The group has had a major impact on the sub-Saharan region of Africa, particularly the Ivory Coast. By 2030, they plan to have universal and equal access to safe and affordable drinking water.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Ivory_Coast" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Ivory Coast">Military of Ivory Coast</a></div>
<p>As of 2012<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, major equipment items reported by the Ivory Coast Army included 10 <a href="/wiki/T-55" class="mw-redirect" title="T-55">T-55</a> tanks (marked as potentially unserviceable), five <a href="/wiki/AMX-13" title="AMX-13">AMX-13</a> light tanks, 34 reconnaissance vehicles, 10 BMP-1/2 armoured infantry fighting vehicles, 41 wheeled APCs, and 36+ artillery pieces.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2012, the Ivory Coast Air Force consisted of one <a href="/wiki/Mil_Mi-24" title="Mil Mi-24">Mil Mi-24</a> attack helicopter and three <a href="/wiki/A%C3%A9rospatiale_SA_330_Puma" title="Aérospatiale SA 330 Puma">SA330L Puma</a> transports (marked as potentially unserviceable).<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2017, Ivory Coast signed the UN <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons" title="Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons">treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Districts_of_C%C3%B4te_d%27Ivoire_(Numbered).png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Districts_of_C%C3%B4te_d%27Ivoire_%28Numbered%29.png/220px-Districts_of_C%C3%B4te_d%27Ivoire_%28Numbered%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="242" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Districts_of_C%C3%B4te_d%27Ivoire_%28Numbered%29.png/330px-Districts_of_C%C3%B4te_d%27Ivoire_%28Numbered%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Districts_of_C%C3%B4te_d%27Ivoire_%28Numbered%29.png/440px-Districts_of_C%C3%B4te_d%27Ivoire_%28Numbered%29.png 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="2197" /></a><figcaption>Districts of Ivory Coast</figcaption></figure>
<p>Since 2011, Ivory Coast has been administratively organised into <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Districts of Ivory Coast">12 districts</a> plus two district-level autonomous cities. The <a href="/wiki/Subdivisions_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Subdivisions of Ivory Coast">districts are sub-divided</a> into <a href="/wiki/Regions_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Regions of Ivory Coast">31 regions</a>; the regions are divided into <a href="/wiki/Departments_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Departments of Ivory Coast">108 departments</a>; and the departments are divided into <a href="/wiki/Sub-prefectures_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Sub-prefectures of Ivory Coast">510 sub-prefectures</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> In some instances, multiple villages are organised into <a href="/wiki/Communes_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Communes of Ivory Coast">communes</a>. The autonomous districts are not divided into regions, but they do contain departments, sub-prefectures, and communes. Since 2011, governors for the 12 non-autonomous districts have not been appointed. As a result, these districts have not yet begun to function as governmental entities.
</p><p>The following is the list of districts, district capitals and each district's regions:
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Geography of Ivory Coast">Geography of Ivory Coast</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_CIV_present.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Koppen-Geiger_Map_CIV_present.svg/300px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_CIV_present.svg.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="219" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Koppen-Geiger_Map_CIV_present.svg/450px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_CIV_present.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Koppen-Geiger_Map_CIV_present.svg/600px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_CIV_present.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1164" data-file-height="850" /></a><figcaption>Köppen climate classification map of Ivory Coast</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ivory Coast is a country in western <a href="/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africa" title="Sub-Saharan Africa">sub-Saharan Africa</a>. It borders <a href="/wiki/Liberia" title="Liberia">Liberia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Guinea" title="Guinea">Guinea</a> in the west, <a href="/wiki/Mali" title="Mali">Mali</a> and <a href="/wiki/Burkina_Faso" title="Burkina Faso">Burkina Faso</a> in the north, <a href="/wiki/Ghana" title="Ghana">Ghana</a> in the east, and the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Guinea" title="Gulf of Guinea">Gulf of Guinea</a> (Atlantic Ocean) in the south. The country lies between latitudes <a href="/wiki/4th_parallel_north" title="4th parallel north">4°</a> and <a href="/wiki/11th_parallel_north" title="11th parallel north">11°N</a>, and longitudes <a href="/wiki/2nd_meridian_west" title="2nd meridian west">2°</a> and <a href="/wiki/9th_meridian_west" title="9th meridian west">9°W</a>. Around 64.8% of the land is agricultural land; arable land amounted to 9.1%, permanent pasture 41.5%, and permanent crops 14.2%. Water pollution is one of the biggest issues that the country is currently facing.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_2-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity">Biodiversity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Biodiversity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Wildlife of Ivory Coast">Wildlife of Ivory Coast</a>, <a href="/wiki/Environment_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Environment of Ivory Coast">Environment of Ivory Coast</a>, and <a href="/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_Ivory_Coast" title="List of national parks of Ivory Coast">List of national parks of Ivory Coast</a></div>
<p>There are over 1,200 animal species including 223 mammals, 702 birds, 125 reptiles, 38 amphibians, and 111 species of fish, alongside 4,700 plant species. It is the most biodiverse country in West Africa, with the majority of its wildlife population living in the nation's rugged interior.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The nation has nine national parks, the largest of which is <a href="/wiki/Assagny_National_Park" title="Assagny National Park">Assgny National Park</a> which occupies an area of around 17,000 hectares or 42,000 acres.<sup id="cite_ref-UNEP_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNEP-95">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country contains six terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Guinean_forests" title="Eastern Guinean forests">Eastern Guinean forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guinean_montane_forests" title="Guinean montane forests">Guinean montane forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Western_Guinean_lowland_forests" title="Western Guinean lowland forests">Western Guinean lowland forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guinean_forest%E2%80%93savanna_mosaic" title="Guinean forest–savanna mosaic">Guinean forest–savanna mosaic</a>, <a href="/wiki/West_Sudanian_savanna" title="West Sudanian savanna">West Sudanian savanna</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Guinean_mangroves" title="Guinean mangroves">Guinean mangroves</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-96">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> It had a 2018 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 3.64/10, ranking it 143rd globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-97">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Economy of Ivory Coast">Economy of Ivory Coast</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cote_D%27ivoire_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/Cote_D%27ivoire_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-Cote_D%27ivoire_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/Cote_D%27ivoire_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-Cote_D%27ivoire_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/Cote_D%27ivoire_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-Cote_D%27ivoire_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a><figcaption>A proportional representation of Ivory Coast, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ivory_Coast.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ivory_Coast.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ivory_Coast.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ivory_Coast.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ivory_Coast.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ivory_Coast.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Ivory_Coast.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>GDP per capita development</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ivory Coast has, for the region, a relatively high <a href="/wiki/Per_capita_income" title="Per capita income">income per capita</a> (US$1,662 in 2017) and plays a key role in transit trade for neighbouring <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked countries</a>. The country is the largest economy in the <a href="/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Economic Community of West African States">West African Economic and Monetary Union</a>, constituting 40% of the monetary union's total GDP. Ivory Coast is the fourth-largest exporter of general goods in sub-Saharan Africa (following South Africa, Nigeria, and Angola).<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country is the world's largest exporter of <a href="/wiki/Cocoa_bean" title="Cocoa bean">cocoa beans</a>. In 2009, cocoa-bean farmers earned $2.53 billion for cocoa exports and were projected to produce 630,000 metric tons in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> Ivory Coast also has 100,000 <a href="/wiki/Rubber" class="mw-redirect" title="Rubber">rubber</a> farmers who earned a total of $105 million in 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Close ties to France since independence in 1960, diversification of agricultural exports, and encouragement of foreign investment have been factors in economic growth. In recent years, Ivory Coast has been subject to greater competition and falling prices in the global marketplace for its primary crops of coffee and cocoa. That, compounded with high internal corruption, makes life difficult for the grower, those exporting into foreign markets, and the labour force; instances of <a href="/wiki/Indentured_servitude" title="Indentured servitude">indentured labour</a> have been reported in the country's cocoa and coffee production in every edition of the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Labor" title="United States Department of Labor">U.S. Department of Labor</a>'s <i>List of Goods Produced by Child Labor or Forced Labor</i> since 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ivory Coast's economy has grown faster than that of most other African countries since independence. One possible reason for this might be taxes on exported agriculture. Ivory Coast, Nigeria, and Kenya were exceptions as their rulers were themselves large cash-crop producers, and the newly independent countries desisted from imposing penal rates of taxation on exported agriculture. As such, their economies did well.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Around 7.5 million people made up the workforce in 2009. The workforce took a hit, especially in the private sector, during the early 2000s with numerous economic crises since 1999. Furthermore, these crises caused companies to close and move locations, especially in the tourism industry, and transit and banking companies. Decreasing job markets posed a huge issue as unemployment rates grew. Unemployment rates raised to 9.4% in 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> Solutions proposed to decrease unemployment included diversifying jobs in small trade. This division of work encouraged farmers and the agricultural sector. Self-employment policy, established by the Ivorian government, allowed for very strong growth in the field with an increase of 142% in seven years from 1995.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Demographics of Ivory Coast">Demographics of Ivory Coast</a></div>
<table class="toccolours" style="width:15em;border-top-width:0;border-spacing: 0;float:right;clear:right;margin:0.5em 0 1em 0.5em;"><caption style="border-top:1px #aaa solid;border-left:1px #aaa <figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Adjamemarche2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Adjamemarche2.jpg/250px-Adjamemarche2.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Adjamemarche2.jpg/375px-Adjamemarche2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Adjamemarche2.jpg/500px-Adjamemarche2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2736" /></a><figcaption>Congestion at a market in Abidjan</figcaption></figure>
<p>According to the December 14, 2021 census, the population was 29,389,150,<sup id="cite_ref-census_2021_110-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census_2021-110">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> up from 22,671,331 at the 2014 census.<sup id="cite_ref-census_2014_109-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census_2014-109">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> The first national census in 1975 counted 6.7 million inhabitants.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> According to a <a href="/wiki/Demographic_and_Health_Surveys" title="Demographic and Health Surveys">Demographic and Health Surveys</a> nationwide survey, the <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> stood at 4.3 children per woman in 2021 (with 3.6 in urban areas and 5.3 in rural areas), down from 5.0 children per woman in 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Languages of Ivory Coast">Languages of Ivory Coast</a></div>
<p>It is estimated that 78 languages are spoken in Ivory Coast.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> French, the official language, is taught in schools and serves as a <a href="/wiki/Lingua_franca" title="Lingua franca">lingua franca</a>. A semi-creolized form of French, known as <a href="/wiki/Nouchi" class="mw-redirect" title="Nouchi">Nouchi</a>, has emerged in Abidjan in recent years and spread among the younger generation.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> One of the most common indigenous languages is <a href="/wiki/Dyula_language" title="Dyula language">Dyula</a>, which acts as a trade language in much of the country, particularly in the north, and is mutually intelligible with other <a href="/wiki/Manding_languages" title="Manding languages">Manding languages</a> widely spoken in neighboring countries.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_groups">Ethnic groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Ethnic groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Ivory_Coast#Ethnic_groups" title="Demographics of Ivory Coast">Demographics of Ivory Coast §&#160;Ethnic groups</a></div>
<p>Macroethnic groupings in the country include <a href="/wiki/Akan_people" title="Akan people">Akan</a> (42.1%), Voltaiques or <a href="/wiki/Gur_languages" title="Gur languages">Gur</a> (17.6%), <a href="/wiki/Mand%C3%A9_peoples" title="Mandé peoples">Northern Mandés</a> (16.5%), <a href="/wiki/Kru_people" title="Kru people">Kru-speaking peoples</a> (11%), <a href="/wiki/Mand%C3%A9_peoples" title="Mandé peoples">Southern Mandés</a> (10%), and others (2.8%, including 100,000 <a href="/wiki/Lebanese_people_in_Ivory_Coast" title="Lebanese people in Ivory Coast">Lebanese</a><sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> and 45,000 French; 2004). Each of these categories is subdivided into different ethnicities. For example, the Akan grouping includes the <a href="/wiki/Baoul%C3%A9_people" title="Baoulé people">Baoulé</a>, the Voltaique category includes the <a href="/wiki/Senufo_people" title="Senufo people">Senufo</a>, the Northern Mande category includes the <a href="/wiki/Dyula_people" title="Dyula people">Dioula</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Mandinka_people" title="Mandinka people">Maninka</a>, the Kru category includes the <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9t%C3%A9_people" title="Bété people">Bété</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Kru_people" title="Kru people">Kru</a>, and the Southern Mande category includes the <a href="/wiki/Dan_people" title="Dan people">Yacouba</a>.
</p><p>About 77% of the population is considered Ivorian. Since Ivory Coast has established itself as one of the most successful West African nations, about 20% of the population (about 3.4&#160; million) consists of workers from neighbouring Liberia, Burkina Faso, and Guinea. About 4% of the population is of non-African ancestry. Many are French,<sup id="cite_ref-csecon_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-csecon-57">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Lebanese,<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Vietnamese and Spanish citizens, as well as <a href="/wiki/Evangelicalism" title="Evangelicalism">evangelical</a> missionaries from the United States and Canada. In November 2004, around 10,000 French and other foreign nationals evacuated Ivory Coast due to attacks from pro-government youth militias.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Aside from French nationals, native-born descendants of French settlers who arrived during the country's colonial period are present.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Ivory_Coast" title="Religion in Ivory Coast">Religion in Ivory Coast</a>, <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Ivory_Coast" title="Islam in Ivory Coast">Islam in Ivory Coast</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Ivory_Coast" title="Christianity in Ivory Coast">Christianity in Ivory Coast</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Notre_dame_de_la_paix_yamoussoukro_by_felix_krohn.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/02/Notre_dame_de_la_paix_yamoussoukro_by_felix_krohn.jpg/220px-Notre_dame_de_la_paix_yamoussoukro_by_felix_krohn.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/02/Notre_dame_de_la_paix_yamoussoukro_by_felix_krohn.jpg/330px-Notre_dame_de_la_paix_yamoussoukro_by_felix_krohn.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/02/Notre_dame_de_la_paix_yamoussoukro_by_felix_krohn.jpg/440px-Notre_dame_de_la_paix_yamoussoukro_by_felix_krohn.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2640" data-file-height="1980" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic</a> Basilica of <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Peace" title="Our Lady of Peace">Our Lady of Peace</a> in <a href="/wiki/Yamoussoukro" title="Yamoussoukro">Yamoussoukro</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ivory Coast is religiously diverse. According to the latest 2021 census data, adherents of <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> (mainly <a href="/wiki/Sunni" class="mw-redirect" title="Sunni">Sunni</a>) represented 42.5% of the total population, while followers of <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> (mainly <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Ivory_Coast" title="Catholic Church in Ivory Coast">Catholic</a> and <a href="/wiki/Evangelical" class="mw-redirect" title="Evangelical">Evangelical</a>) comprised 39.8% of the population. An additional 12.6% of the population identified as <a href="/wiki/Irreligious" class="mw-redirect" title="Irreligious">Irreligious</a>, while 2.2% reported following <a href="/wiki/Animism" title="Animism">Animism</a> (<a href="/wiki/Traditional_African_religions" title="Traditional African religions">traditional African religions</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-:religions2021_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:religions2021-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A 2020 estimate by the Pew Research Center, projected that Christians would represent 44% of the total population, while Muslims would represent 37.2% of the population. In addition, it estimated that 8.1% would be religiously unaffiliated, and 10.5% as followers of traditional African religions (animism).<sup id="cite_ref-globalrf_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-globalrf-120">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-CIA_2-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> In 2009, according to <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State" title="United States Department of State">U.S. Department of State</a> estimates, Christians and Muslims each made up 35% to 40% of the population, while an estimated 25% of the population practised traditional (animist) religions.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Yamoussoukro is home to the largest church building in the world, the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Our_Lady_of_Peace" title="Basilica of Our Lady of Peace">Basilica of Our Lady of Peace</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Ivory_Coast" title="Health in Ivory Coast">Health in Ivory Coast</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">Life expectancy</a> at birth was 42 for males in 2004; for females it was 47.<sup id="cite_ref-afro.who.int_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-afro.who.int-123">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Infant_mortality" title="Infant mortality">Infant mortality</a> was 118 of 1000 live births.<sup id="cite_ref-afro.who.int_123-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-afro.who.int-123">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> Twelve physicians are available per 100,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-afro.who.int_123-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-afro.who.int-123">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> About a quarter of the population lives below the international poverty line of US$1.25 a day.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> About 36% of women have undergone <a href="/wiki/Female_genital_mutilation" title="Female genital mutilation">female genital mutilation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> According to 2010 estimates, Ivory Coast has the 27th-highest <a href="/wiki/Maternal_death" title="Maternal death">maternal mortality</a> rate in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> The HIV/AIDS rate was 19th-highest in the world, estimated in 2012 at 3.20% among adults aged 15–49 years.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Ivory_Coast" title="Education in Ivory Coast">Education in Ivory Coast</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Universit%C3%A9_Abidjan_1.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/Universit%C3%A9_Abidjan_1.JPG/220px-Universit%C3%A9_Abidjan_1.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/Universit%C3%A9_Abidjan_1.JPG/330px-Universit%C3%A9_Abidjan_1.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/Universit%C3%A9_Abidjan_1.JPG/440px-Universit%C3%A9_Abidjan_1.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="2664" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/University_campus" class="mw-redirect" title="University campus">university campus</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Universit%C3%A9_de_Cocody" class="mw-redirect" title="Université de Cocody">Université de Cocody</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Among sub-Saharan African countries, Ivory Coast has one of the highest literacy rates.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_2-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/The_World_Factbook" title="The World Factbook">The World Factbook</a> as of 2019, 89.9% of the population aged 15 and over can read and write.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> A large part of the adult population, in particular women, is illiterate. Many children between 6 and 10 years old are not enrolled in school.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> The majority of students in secondary education are male. At the end of secondary education, students can sit for the<i> baccalauréat</i> examination. Universities include <a href="/wiki/Universit%C3%A9_F%C3%A9lix_Houphou%C3%ABt-Boigny" title="Université Félix Houphouët-Boigny">Université Félix Houphouët-Boigny</a> in Abidjan and the <a href="/wiki/Universit%C3%A9_Alassane_Ouattara" title="Université Alassane Ouattara">Université Alassane Ouattara</a> in Bouaké. 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Science_and_technology">Science and technology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Science and technology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Ivory_Coast" title="Science and technology in Ivory Coast">Science and technology in Ivory Coast</a></div>
<p>According to the Ministry of Higher Education and Scientific Research, Ivory Coast devotes about 0.13% of GDP to GERD. Apart from low investment, other challenges include inadequate scientific equipment, the fragmentation of research organizations and a failure to exploit and protect research results.<sup id="cite_ref-UNESCO_science_report_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNESCO_science_report-130">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Ivory Coast was ranked 114st in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2021, down from 103rd in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The share of the <i>National Development Plan</i> for 2012–2015 that is devoted to scientific research remains modest. Within the section on greater wealth creation and social equity (63.8% of the total budget for the <i>Plan</i>), just 1.2% is allocated to scientific research. Twenty-four national research programmes group public and private research and training institutions around a common research theme. These programmes correspond to eight priority sectors for 2012–2015, namely: health, raw materials, agriculture, culture, environment, governance, mining and energy; and technology.<sup id="cite_ref-UNESCO_science_report_130-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNESCO_science_report-130">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Culture of Ivory Coast">Culture of Ivory Coast</a>, <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Ivory_Coast" title="Music of Ivory Coast">Music of Ivory Coast</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Ivory_Coast" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Ivory Coast">Media of Ivory Coast</a></div>
<p>Each of the ethnic groups in the Ivory Coast has its own music genres, most showing strong vocal <a href="/wiki/Polyphony" title="Polyphony">polyphony</a>. <a href="/wiki/Talking_drum" title="Talking drum">Talking drums</a> are common, especially among the <a href="/wiki/Nzema_people" title="Nzema people">Appolo</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Polyrhythm" title="Polyrhythm">polyrhythms</a>, another African characteristic, are found throughout Ivory Coast and are especially common in the southwest. Popular music genres from Ivory Coast include <a href="/wiki/Zoblazo" title="Zoblazo">zoblazo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zouglou" title="Zouglou">zouglou</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Coup%C3%A9-D%C3%A9cal%C3%A9" class="mw-redirect" title="Coupé-Décalé">Coupé-Décalé</a>. A few Ivorian artists who have known international success are <a href="/wiki/Magic_System" title="Magic System">Magic Système</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alpha_Blondy" title="Alpha Blondy">Alpha Blondy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Meiway" title="Meiway">Meiway</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dobet_Gnahor%C3%A9" title="Dobet Gnahoré">Dobet Gnahoré</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tiken_Jah_Fakoly" title="Tiken Jah Fakoly">Tiken Jah Fakoly</a>, <a href="/wiki/DJ_Arafat" title="DJ Arafat">DJ Arafat</a>, AfroB, <a href="/wiki/Serge_Beynaud" title="Serge Beynaud">Serge Beynaud</a> and <a href="/wiki/Christina_Goh" title="Christina Goh">Christina Goh</a>, of Ivorian descent.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast_at_the_Olympics" title="Ivory Coast at the Olympics">Ivory Coast at the Olympics</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cote_d%27Iviore_NT_2010.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Cote_d%27Iviore_NT_2010.jpg/220px-Cote_d%27Iviore_NT_2010.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Cote_d%27Iviore_NT_2010.jpg/330px-Cote_d%27Iviore_NT_2010.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Cote_d%27Iviore_NT_2010.jpg/440px-Cote_d%27Iviore_NT_2010.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1067" /></a><figcaption><div class="center" style="width:auto; margin-left:auto; margin-right:auto;">The <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast_national_football_team" title="Ivory Coast national football team">Ivory Coast national football team</a></div></figcaption></figure>
<p>The most popular sport is <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">association football</a>. The men's <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast_national_football_team" title="Ivory Coast national football team">national football team</a> has played in the World Cup three times, <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast_at_the_FIFA_World_Cup" title="Ivory Coast at the FIFA World Cup">in Germany 2006, in South Africa 2010, and Brazil in 2014</a>. Côte d'Ivoire has produced many well-known footballers like <a href="/wiki/Didier_Drogba" title="Didier Drogba">Didier Drogba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Yaya_Tour%C3%A9" title="Yaya Touré">Yaya Touré</a>. The women's football team played in the 2015 Women's World Cup in Canada. The country has been the host of several major African sporting events, with the most recent being the <a href="/wiki/AfroBasket_2013" title="AfroBasket 2013">2013 African Basketball Championship</a>. In the past, the country hosted the <a href="/wiki/1984_African_Cup_of_Nations" title="1984 African Cup of Nations">1984 African Cup of Nations</a>, in which the <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast_national_football_team" title="Ivory Coast national football team">Ivory Coast football team</a> finished fifth, and the <a href="/wiki/FIBA_Africa_Championship_1985" title="FIBA Africa Championship 1985">1985 African Basketball Championship</a>, where the <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast_men%27s_national_basketball_team" title="Ivory Coast men&#39;s national basketball team">national basketball team</a> won the gold medal.
</p><p>400m metre runner <a href="/wiki/Gabriel_Tiacoh" title="Gabriel Tiacoh">Gabriel Tiacoh</a> won the silver medal in the <a href="/wiki/Athletics_at_the_1984_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_400_metres" title="Athletics at the 1984 Summer Olympics – Men&#39;s 400 metres">men's 400 metres</a> at the 1984 Olympics. The country hosted the <a href="/wiki/2017_Jeux_de_la_Francophonie" title="2017 Jeux de la Francophonie">8th edition</a> of <a href="/wiki/Jeux_de_la_Francophonie" title="Jeux de la Francophonie">Jeux de la Francophonie</a> in 2017. In the <a href="/wiki/Sport_of_athletics" title="Sport of athletics">sport of athletics</a>, well known participants include <a href="/wiki/Marie-Jos%C3%A9e_Ta_Lou" title="Marie-Josée Ta Lou">Marie-Josée Ta Lou</a> and <a href="/wiki/Murielle_Ahour%C3%A9" class="mw-redirect" title="Murielle Ahouré">Murielle Ahouré</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Rugby_union" title="Rugby union">Rugby union</a> is popular, and the <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast_national_rugby_union_team" title="Ivory Coast national rugby union team">national rugby union team</a> qualified to play at the <a href="/wiki/1995_Rugby_World_Cup" title="1995 Rugby World Cup">Rugby World Cup</a> in South Africa in 1995. Ivory Coast has won two Africa Cups: one in 1992 and the other in 2015. Ivory Coast is known for <a href="/wiki/Taekwondo" title="Taekwondo">Taekwondo</a> with well-known competitors such as <a href="/wiki/Cheick_Sallah_Ciss%C3%A9" title="Cheick Sallah Cissé">Cheick Cissé</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ruth_Gbagbi" title="Ruth Gbagbi">Ruth Gbagbi</a>, and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Firmin_Zokou&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Firmin Zokou (page does not exist)">Firmin Zokou</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Yassapoulet.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Yassapoulet.JPG/220px-Yassapoulet.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Yassapoulet.JPG/330px-Yassapoulet.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Yassapoulet.JPG/440px-Yassapoulet.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2816" data-file-height="2112" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Yassa_(food)" title="Yassa (food)"><i>Yassa</i></a> is a popular dish throughout West Africa prepared with chicken or fish. Chicken yassa is pictured.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Ivorian_cuisine" title="Ivorian cuisine">Ivorian cuisine</a></div>
<p>Traditional cuisine is very similar to that of neighbouring countries in West Africa in its reliance on grains and tubers. <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">Cassava</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cooking_banana" title="Cooking banana">plantains</a> are significant parts of Ivorian cuisine. A type of corn paste called <i>aitiu</i> is used to prepare corn balls, and peanuts are widely used in many dishes. <i><a href="/wiki/Acheke" title="Acheke">Attiéké</a></i> is a popular side dish made with grated cassava, a vegetable-based <a href="/wiki/Couscous" title="Couscous">couscous</a>. Common street food is <i><a href="/wiki/Alloco" class="mw-redirect" title="Alloco">alloco</a></i>, plantain fried in <a href="/wiki/Palm_oil" title="Palm oil">palm oil</a>, spiced with steamed onions and chili, and eaten along with grilled fish or boiled eggs. Chicken is commonly consumed and has a unique flavor because of its lean, low-fat mass in this region. Seafood includes tuna, sardines, shrimp, and <a href="/wiki/Bonito" title="Bonito">bonito</a>, which is similar to tuna. <i><a href="/wiki/Peanut_stew" title="Peanut stew">Mafé</a></i> is a common dish consisting of meat in peanut sauce.<sup id="cite_ref-:10_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-135">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Slow-simmered stews with various ingredients are another common food staple.<sup id="cite_ref-:10_135-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-135">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/Kedjenou" title="Kedjenou">Kedjenou</a></i> is a dish consisting of chicken and vegetables slow-cooked in a sealed pot with little or no added liquid, which concentrates the flavors of the chicken and vegetables and tenderizes the chicken.<sup id="cite_ref-:10_135-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-135">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> It is usually cooked in a pottery jar called a canary, over a slow fire, or cooked in an oven.<sup id="cite_ref-:10_135-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-135">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> <i>Bangui</i> is a local <a href="/wiki/Palm_wine" title="Palm wine">palm wine</a>.
</p><p>Ivorians have a particular kind of small, open-air restaurant called a <i>maquis</i>, which is unique to the region. A <i>maquis</i> normally features braised chicken, and fish covered in onions and tomatoes served with <i>attiéké</i> or <i>kedjenou</i>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ivory_Coast&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1011085734"><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-8"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-8">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The latter being pronounced <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span></span><span class="wrap"> </span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;v&#39; in &#39;vie&#39;">v</span><span title="&#39;w&#39; in &#39;wind&#39;">w</span><span title="/ɑːr/: &#39;ar&#39; in &#39;far&#39;">ɑːr</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">KOHT</span> dee-<span style="font-size:90%">VWAR</span></i></a> in English and <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="fr-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/French" title="Help:IPA/French">[kot divwaʁ]</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/5\/5d\/Fr-C%C3%B4te-d%27Ivoire-fr-Paris.ogg\/Fr-C%C3%B4te-d%27Ivoire-fr-Paris.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;Fr-C\u00f4te-d&#39;Ivoire-fr-Paris.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/5/5d/Fr-C%C3%B4te-d%27Ivoire-fr-Paris.ogg/Fr-C%C3%B4te-d%27Ivoire-fr-Paris.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Fr-C%C3%B4te-d%27Ivoire-fr-Paris.ogg" title="File:Fr-Côte-d&#39;Ivoire-fr-Paris.ogg">i</a></sup> in French.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-teeths-20"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-teeths_20-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Joseph_Vaiss%C3%A8te" title="Joseph Vaissète">Joseph Vaissète</a>, in his 1755 <i>Géographie historique, ecclésiastique et civile</i>, lists the name as <i>La Côte des Dents</i> ("The Coast of the Teeth"), but notes that <i>Côte de Dents</i> is the more correct form.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVaissète1755185–186_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaissète1755185–186-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-vent-24"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-vent_24-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><i>Côte du Vent</i> sometimes denoted the combined "Ivory" and "Grain" coasts, or sometimes just the "Grain" coast.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuckett1853594_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuckett1853594-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThornton199653–56_14-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThornton199653–56-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-trans-27"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-trans_27-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Literal translations include <i>Elfenbeinküste</i> (German), <i>Costa d'Avorio</i> (Italian), <i>Norsunluurannikko</i> (Finnish), Бе́рег Слоно́вой Ко́сти (Russian), and Ivory Coast.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavid20007_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavid20007-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-organizations-36"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-organizations_36-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Many governments use "Côte d'Ivoire" for diplomatic reasons, as do their outlets, such as the Chinese CCTV News. Other organizations that use "Côte d'Ivoire" include the <a href="/wiki/Central_Intelligence_Agency" title="Central Intelligence Agency">Central Intelligence Agency</a> in its <i><a href="/wiki/The_World_Factbook" title="The World Factbook">World Factbook</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-CIA_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> and the international sport organizations <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a><sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/International_Olympic_Committee" title="International Olympic Committee">IOC</a><sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> (referring to their <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast_national_football_team" title="Ivory Coast national football team">national football</a> and Olympic teams in international games and in official broadcasts), news magazine <i><a href="/wiki/The_Economist" title="The Economist">The Economist</a></i>,<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> the <i><a href="/wiki/Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica" title="Encyclopædia Britannica">Encyclopædia Britannica</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> and the <i><a href="/wiki/National_Geographic_Society" title="National Geographic Society">National Geographic Society</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-outlets-39"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-outlets_39-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The <a href="/wiki/BBC" title="BBC">BBC</a> usually uses "Ivory Coast" both in news reports and on its page about the country.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/The_Guardian" title="The Guardian">The Guardian</a></i> newspaper's style guide says: "Ivory Coast, not 'The Ivory Coast' or 'Côte d'Ivoire'; its nationals are Ivorians."<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
<span class="citation-needed-content" style="padding-left:0.1em; padding-right:0.1em; color:#595959; border:1px solid #DDD;"><a href="/wiki/ABC_News" title="ABC News">ABC News</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fox_News" title="Fox News">Fox News</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Times" title="The Times">The Times</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times">The New York Times</a></i>, the <a href="/wiki/South_African_Broadcasting_Corporation" class="mw-redirect" title="South African Broadcasting Corporation">South African Broadcasting Corporation</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/CBC_News" title="CBC News">Canadian Broadcasting Corporation</a> all use "Ivory Coast" either exclusively or predominantly.</span><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
